# Unallocated R11 lead: a non-diagonal Brownian-bridge remainder for dominance

**UNVERIFIED manual algebra. No full certificate, model allocation, mathematical program or formal submission. Original target OPEN.** This note preserves a genuinely non-diagonal remainder and changes the function-value weights; the positive-diagonal interval dictionary excluded in R11_VARIABLE_INTERVAL_CERTIFICATE_LEAD_20261009.md does not automatically cover it. Its actual coefficient/positivity problem is UNSOLVED.

## 1. Exact original-radius dominance residual

Fix an arbitrary physical word h_0,...,h_(n-1)>=0 and its actual normalized smooth-convex trajectory. All sampled gradients include the actual terminalg_n. Use

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||²>=0,
    Q_star,i=2fstar-2f_i+2<g_i,x_i-xstar>-||g_i||²>=0.

Choose fixed nonnegative weightsu_0,...,u_n with totalB=sum u_i>0. This local B is a certificate weight, distinct from the original benchmark B_n. Define the doubled dominance residual

    D_u=||x0-xstar||²-sum_i u_i Q_star,i
        -2B(f_n-fstar)-||x0-xstar-sum_i u_i g_i||².

It is exactly

    D_u=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_n)
         +sum_i u_i(1-u_i)||g_i||²
         +2sum_(j<i)u_i(h_j-u_j)<g_i,g_j>.             (1)

Every j<i satisfiesj<n, so h_j in(1) is always an actual physical step. If D_u>=0 for ALL actual smooth-convex trajectories, then nonnegativity ofQ_star,i and of the final square gives the full-class bound W_n(h)<=1/B, at the original selected minimizer/radius. No such nonnegativity is assumed.

## 2. A covariance that represents the cross-gradient terms exactly

Choose parameters

    1>rho_0>rho_1>...>rho_n>0,
    u_i=rho_i h_i for i<n.

The strict ordering is convenient for a nonsingular covariance; weak ordering is possible with degeneracies treated explicitly. Chooseu_n>0 separately. Introduce scalar weightsell_i=h_i fori<n andell_n=u_n/rho_n. The LASTell_n is solely an algebraic weight, NOT a physical step or another update.

Define the matrix

    K_ij=ell_i ell_j [min(rho_i,rho_j)-rho_i rho_j].   (2)

It is positive semidefinite, since

    sum_(i,j)K_ij<g_i,g_j>
      =integral_0^1 ||sum_i ell_i g_i
                   (1_(t<=rho_i)-rho_i)||² dt >=0.   (3)

This is the covariance of a centered Bernoulli-threshold process, often called the Brownian-bridge covariance. Formula(3) proves the PSD statement directly; no external stochastic theorem is needed.

Fori>j, rho_i<rho_j implies

    K_ij=ell_i ell_j rho_i(1-rho_j)=u_i(h_j-u_j).

Its diagonal isK_ii=u_i(ell_i-u_i). Therefore the exact same functional(1) can be written

    D_u=2sum_(i<n)u_i(f_i-f_n)
        +sum_i u_i(1-ell_i)||g_i||²
        +sum_(i,j)K_ij<g_i,g_j>.                     (4)

This does not replace the physical gradients by random ones. The covariance is a deterministic positive matrix acting on the ORIGINAL queried gradient vectors.

## 3. Combine with original interpolation rows without forcing diagonal slack

Let Bmat be the actual trajectory matrix with diagonal1 and Bmat_ij=h_j forj<i. Suppose C is a zero-row-sum matrix with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sums

    b_i=u_i fori<n, b_n=-sum_(i<n)u_i=u_n-B.

Then, usinglambda_ij=C_ji fori!=j,

    sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij
       =2sum_i b_i f_i+g^T(H-diag(b))g,
    H=C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T.

Consequently the EXACT remaining matrix is

    D_u=sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij+g^T R g,
    R=K+diag(u_i(1-ell_i)+b_i)-H.                    (5)

For a word satisfying the companion interval modes' h_i>=1 assumptions, those modes make H diagonal, soR=K+diag(mu), but the diagonalmu is NOT required to be nonnegative. The complete R must be proved PSD. This distinction matters: imposingmu>=0 throws away the non-diagonal mechanism and returns to a much smaller method.

Formula(3) gives an exact alternative route to that matrix proof: partition[0,1] at the rho_i, express the integral as a finite positive sum of squared original gradient partial sums, and compare it against the signed diagonalmu. A valid proof would be a weighted discrete inequality on ALL vectors; no independence or freedom of actual histories is used to assert a counterexample if that sufficient test fails.

## 4. Direct relation to the desired complete-H_m upper target

For S=sum H_m, set the desired total weight

    B_target=1+(32/17)S.

For any selected decreasingrho_i in(0,1), the choice

    u_n=B_target-sum_(i<n)rho_i h_i

is positive, because B_target>S. A successful explicitC and fullPSDproof for(5), uniformly over every sufficiently large inheritedm, would give

    W(H_m)<=1/B_target<=17/[16(1+2S)].

The accepted benchmark ratio would then close the original negative direction. No suchrho profile, C array or PSD estimate is given in this note. The mass identity by itself is only bookkeeping.

The positive A/D conic-hull obstruction in the companion note fixed physical function weightsb_i=h_i and imposed nonnegative physical diagonal slack. Hereb_i=rho_i h_i andthe FULLnon-diagonal matrixR is kept. That old obstruction cannot simply be reused without a new derivation. Conversely, a named covariance does not prove the new family feasible.

The R3 weighted-prefix report tested two PARTICULAR outgoing minimizer-row laws for a different last-prefix objective. Its full matrix obstruction must be respected at those exact profiles; it is not a blanket exclusion of allu in(1). The old R2 history family, R3 source laws, and the present original-terminal dominance functional must be matched explicitly before claiming novelty or impossibility.

A future allocation should first choose an actualrho law and matchingC, then prove the full original-space inequality or reject that precise law using an exact original-space obstruction. It should not stop at the equivalent feasibility statement(5), and must not invoke an unallocated solver. No new native, mathematical-program or formal allowance follows from this note.

# R11 parent supplement: finite left expression and endpoint-normal retention

**UNVERIFIED manual derivation. No all-depth inequality is proved.** Keep all notation and actual sample conditions of R11_PARENT_INPUT_NORMAL_TRANSPORT_NOTE_20261009.md. This supplement does not alter that frozen source's hash or any native grant. No mathematical program was used.

## 1. The left quadratic is independent of the feasible comparison parameter

Suppose the child and proposed parent canonical fractions are the same eta, with chi=1-eta. Write Delta=alpha-b, w0=M p0-A ell, and e=p-r. After inserting the child lower bound EL>=eta RL+gamma BL into the full R10 parent identity, and transporting the input normals as in the earlier note, retain the square chi*M²||p0-ztheta||².

Since

    M(p0-ztheta)=w0+(1-theta)A u+Delta p-(D+sigma_k h)r,
    eN=nR-n0=w0+Au+Delta p-Dr,
    D+sigma_k h-Delta=M-A,

all theta dependence cancels from the resulting finite LEFT expression. With

    R0=w0-(M-A)r=M(p0-r)-A(ell-r),

that expression is exactly

    Q_left=chi||R0||²-chi Delta²||p-r||²
           -2chi Delta sigma_k h<r,p-r>
           -2chi A(M-A)<r,u>.                         (1)

The terms excluded from the label Q_left are: A*gamma*BL; the bridge quadratic Theta||p-r||²; bridge normals with Nr coefficient reduced from hC/(D+k) to hC/(D+k)-chiDelta; the remaining positive input normals

    2chi[A N0(ell)+(M-A)N0(r)];

both signed discount squares; and the complete right-child contribution. Equation(1) must not be treated as a standalone nonnegative quantity. Its final inner product remains substantive.

One way to check (1) is to put Kvec=Delta p-(D+sigma_k h)r. Before completing the square, the relevant terms are

    chi||w0+Kvec||²+2chi A<Kvec,u>
                         -2chi Delta<eN,p-r>.

Expanding eN and using D+sigma_k h-Delta=M-A gives (1). This identity was independently confirmed in a progress message from the R11 upper derivation; no verifier acceptance is implied.

After the actual right-normal payment, the finite right expression for the same eta,gamma is

    Q_right=eta[2kappa hD||uR||²+2D sigma_k h<uR,q>
                       -sigma_k²h²(||q||²-||r||²)]
              +gamma MD||q-r||².                       (2)

The right physical normal rows retain positive coefficients. Equations(1)--(2) expose the full vector debt rather than imposing independent artificial remainder values. A quantitative part of the exact left gap may be needed to control<r,u>.

## 2. The parent endpoint normal can also be retained at the same small fraction

The complete parent endpoint comparison remainder is

    B_all=Rem(x,M,q)=M||p0-q||²+2N0(q).

It has the exact normal transport

    N0(q)=N0(r)+N_Rq+<eN,q-r>,
    N_Rq=<nR,r-q>>=0.                                 (3)

Indeed n0=nR-eN and p0-q=(p0-r)+(r-q). Thus subtracting the SAME endpoint fraction gamma*M*B_all adds the finite terms

    -gamma M²||p0-q||²-2gamma M<eN,q-r>,                (4)

and changes the coefficient of N0(r) to

    2[chi(M-A)-gamma M].                               (5)

The R10 right-normal payment had coefficient2eta h d/D+2gamma M onN_Rq, so its remaining coefficient after(3) is exactly2eta h d/D>=0. No extra loss of the endpoint fraction is needed merely to pay this normal.

For every genuine parent with a nonempty inherited child j>=1, the leaf estimate h_leaf>4/3 gives s>=h_leaf and kappa>=16/17, hence A=1+2kappa s>3. Also h=s+b>s and d>=1 imply M>2A-1. Therefore

    A/M < A/(2A-1) <3/5,
    1-A/M>2/5.

Consequently gamma<=(2/5)chi is a uniform sufficient scalar condition for (5). In particular eta=3/4,gamma=1/1200 satisfies it by a wide margin. The empty-child physical leaf is already covered by the independently derived base and is not silently included in this parent estimate.

This establishes only compatibility of the endpoint NORMAL coefficients. The full quadratic obtained from (1), (2), (4), the child endpoint remainder, the signed discount terms, and all remaining actual projection relations is still unproved. Canonical and endpoint retention at every level do not follow from this calculation alone.

# R11 parent: retain the true initial maximizer to transport the left normal fan

**UNVERIFIED manual derivation. Original target OPEN.** This note changes the R10 allocation by using the individual actual rows inside the parent maximization remainder, rather than discarding that remainder as a nonnegative whole. It does not establish positivity of the remaining quadratic expression or an all-depth U law. No mathematical program or model call produced this note.

Use the exact actual parent notation of research/full_right_gap_u_closure_20261009_r10/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md. One compact convex K contains0; every P denotes its Euclidean projection. Fix kappa in[16/17,1], delta=1-kappa, sigma_k=2kappa-1, d>=1, childmasss, bridgeb=1+k, h=s+b,C=1+s,

    A=1+2kappa s, alpha=A-s, D=d+2kappa s, M=D+2kappa h.

The actual left child is x->v, its gradientspoints areg_i and displacementG=x-v=sum_left h_i g_i. The bridgep=P(v) givesw=v-bp. The right childw->y hasr=P(w/D),q=P(y/d). The left auxiliary projection isell=P(x/A), withnL=x-Aell, zL=(G+alpha p)/A,u=ell-zL.

Now explicitly KEEP the true parent initial maximizer

    p0=P(x/M), n0=x-M p0, w0=nL-n0=M p0-Aell.

All these points and normals are actual samples of the same body. For z inK setN0(z)=<n0,p0-z>>=0. Let

    Fan0=sum_left h_i N0(g_i),
    ztheta=[(1-theta)G+(b-theta alpha)p+(M-h)r+theta Aell]/M,
    Rtheta=Rem(x,M,ztheta).

The guaranteed feasible range remains0<=theta<=min(1,b/alpha). Thus its exact normal part is

    M Rtheta=M²||p0-ztheta||²
       +2[(1-theta)Fan0+(b-theta alpha)N0(p)
           +(M-h)N0(r)+theta A N0(ell)].                  (1)

The old left canonical remainder has RL=A||u||²+2<nL,u>. In the R10 parent identity, after inserting the left-child canonical fraction eta and subtracting the SAME parent fraction eta, the remaining coefficient of<nL,u> is

    -2(1-eta)(1-theta)A<nL,u>.

R10's loss condition arose by treating the normal part of(1) merely as nonnegative. Its rows can instead be used exactly. Putchi=1-eta. SinceG+alpha p=A(ell-u),

    A<nL,u>=Fan0+alpha N0(p)-A N0(ell)+A<w0,u>.      (2)

Therefore the sum of the ENTIRE normal part ofchi*M*Rtheta and this left normal debt is exactly

    2chi[A N0(ell)+(b-alpha)N0(p)+(M-h)N0(r)
                          -(1-theta)A<w0,u>].          (3)

In particular the fan over EVERY original left query cancels, with no shrinking ofeta. This cancellation uses both true initial projections; it is not justified if p0 is replaced by a convenient comparison point.

LetDelta=alpha-b and retain the actual bridge normal

    nR=w-D r, Nr=<nR,r-p>>=0,
    eN=nR-n0=w0+Au+Delta p-D r.

The exact relationN0(p)-N0(r)=Nr-<eN,r-p> turns(3) into

    2chi[A N0(ell)+(M-A)N0(r)-Delta Nr
           +Delta<eN,r-p>-(1-theta)A<w0,u>].           (4)

HereM-A=d-1+2kappa h>0. Thus the only possibly negative normal coefficient newly exposed by this transport is the root coefficient ofNr. All other new terms outside normal cones are explicit finite-vector quadratic expressions.

In the full parent identity the bridge rows originally have coefficients

    2Kp Np+2Kr0 Nr,
    Np=<v-p,p-r>>=0,
    Kp=h(1+C/(D+k)), Kr0=hC/(D+k).

After(4), the new Nr coefficient is2Kr, with

    Kr=hC/(D+k)-chi Delta.

It is NOT uniformly nonnegative for alld: whenDelta>0 andeta<1, the first term tends0 asd grows. Do not claim that the root coefficient always pays it directly.

There is nevertheless an exact all-d transport of this remaining normal. Let e=p-r. The two ACTUAL bridge normals obey

    Np+Nr=<k p+D r,p-r>=(D+k)<p,e>-D||e||².         (5)

IfKr<0, useNr=(Np+Nr)-Np. Then

    2Kp Np+2Kr Nr
      =2(h+chi Delta)Np
          +2Kr[(D+k)<p,e>-D||e||²].                (6)

The coefficienth+chi Delta is positive: ifDelta>=0 this is immediate, while ifDelta<0, h+chiDelta>=h+Delta=s+alpha>0. IfKr>=0, both original remaining bridge normal coefficients are already nonnegative and may instead be retained. Thus no unbounded left-fan or root-normal debt needs to be left as an independent scalar. The price is explicit quadratic terms involving p0,ell,u,p,r; their sign has NOT been established.

This calculation does not consume the squarechi*M²||p0-ztheta||² in(1), the positive retained child squares, the exact right-child gap, or the nonnegative normals in(4). They must be included when testing a complete same-fraction or richer induction. A coefficient matrix with freely varying vectors is only a sufficient screen; a negative direction does not itself give a realizable counterexample.

The previous R10 right-normal payment remains available with its exact actual convex weights. Any parent retention of the endpoint comparison remainder B needs its OWN transport and cannot be assumed from the canonical remainder calculation above. Similarlyeta3/4 andgamma1/1200 are only a proved-in-draft leaf base, not a higher-level assertion.

The concrete next proof obligation is to combine(1)--(6) with the complete R10 parent identity, preserve the actual radial/bridge/right-child projection relations, and prove a recursively sufficient bound at finalkappa>=16/17. The cancellation removes the specific expanding-normal-loss argument; it does not certify the full inequality or the original minimax theorem.

# Unallocated R11 source lead: positive interval modes for a variable physical word

**UNVERIFIED manual derivation; the positive interval dictionary is REFUTED at its stated certificate scope by Section5. No model/program allocation or target certificate.** The source check was for proof methods, not a posted solution of the exact original benchmark. The original Goal remains OPEN. No new mathematical execution was used.

[Jinze Zhao, Constant Steps Are s-Composable, arXiv:2609.11784v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11784v1), Sections3.1--3.3, constructs positive interval-supported interpolation matrices whose product with a constant-step trajectory matrix has diagonal symmetric part. Its theorem concerns a specially balanced CONSTANT schedule; it does not establish a variable-word or perturbed-Silver theorem. The identities below are a manual variable-step extension of that matrix mechanism, not an import of its positivity theorem for combined weights.

## 1. Exact matrix mode for an arbitrary positive word

Let the physical word be h_0,...,h_(n-1), with h_i>=1 for this mode construction. Let B be the (n+1)-square trajectory matrix

    B_ij=h_j for j<i, B_ii=1, B_ij=0 for j>i.

Fix a contiguous interval [a,b] of query indices, a<b. Choose positive weights z_i,v_i for i in[a,b], and set

    Z_i=sum_(a<=k<=i) z_k,  Z_i^-=sum_(a<=k<i) z_k,
    V_i^+=sum_(i<k<=b) v_k, V_i=v_i+V_i^+,
    u_i=h_i Z_i-z_i, w_i=v_i+h_i V_i^+.

The value h_b is irrelevant wherever b is the terminal query: u_b has no upper entries and w_b=v_b. It can be given any dummy value without changing the matrix. Define

    C_ij=u_i v_j for a<=i<j<=b,
    C_ij=w_i z_j for a<=j<i<=b,
    C_ii=-sum_(j!=i) C_ij,

with entries outside the interval zero. Every off-diagonal entry is nonnegative, because u_i=h_i Z_i^-+(h_i-1)z_i>=0 and w_i>0. Every row sums tozero.

For i<j in the interval,

    (CB)_ij=u_i(v_j+h_j V_j^+)=u_i w_j,
    (CB)_ji=w_j(z_i-h_i Z_i)=-w_j u_i.

The second equality uses the zero row sum. Entries of CB outside the interval vanish: earlier columns are h_j times a zero row sum, and later columns are zero by triangularity. Therefore H=CB+B^T C^T is DIAGONAL, with

    H_ii=2[(h_i-1)^2 Z_i V_i^+ - Z_i^- V_i].        (1)

For i=b this is -2 Z_b^- v_b, independent of the dummy h_b. To check(1),

    (CB)_ii=(h_i-1)u_i V_i^+ - w_i Z_i^-
            =(h_i-1)^2 Z_i V_i^+ - Z_i^- V_i.

With constant h and z=v=1 this reproduces the source's interval mode. For variable h it keeps the ACTUAL physical trajectory matrix, not a reparameterized clock. No smoothness or interpolation condition is changed.

## 2. Exact smooth-convex inequality represented by the mode

Let bvec denote the column-sum vector of C, so sum bvec=0. For actual normalized smooth-convex data define

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||²>=0.

Then lambda_ij=C_ji>=0 for i!=j, and the exact identity is

    sum_(i,j) C_ji Q_ij
       =2 sum_i bvec_i f_i + sum_i(H_ii-bvec_i)||g_i||².   (2)

Indeed beta_i=(Bg)_i=g_i+x0-x_i gives

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j+2<g_j,beta_i-beta_j>-||g_i||²+||g_j||².

Summing the full zero-row-sum C, including its harmless diagonal sinceQ_ii=0, eliminates the beta_j terms and leaves2g^T C B g. Equation(1) makes this diagonal. This also fixes the transpose convention; using C_ij rather thanC_ji as the interpolation multiplier would give a different expression.

Positive sums of these modes remain valid. However, no mode weights have been constructed that match the actual original-target objective on H_m. Nonnegativity of EACH mode does not imply nonnegativity of the coefficients needed to combine them.

## 3. A concrete sufficient whole-word target, not a claimed solution

For mass S=sum h and kappa=16/17, let

    sigma=2kappa-1=15/17, tau=1+sigma S,
    B_terminal=tau(tau-1)=sigma S(1+sigma S).

The actual smooth-convex history expression

    E_history=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
              -sum_(i<n)h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||²
              -B_terminal||g_n||²                         (3)

would be nonnegative if an explicit nonnegative combination of modes yielded column sums

    bvec_i=h_i for i<n, bvec_n=-S,

and diagonal entries satisfying

    H_ii=h_i(2-h_i)-mu_i for i<n,
    H_nn=-S-B_terminal-mu_n,
    mu_i>=0 for EVERY index.

Then(2) plus sum mu_i||g_i||² equals(3). This is a checkable sufficient coefficient target with real physical steps. It is NOT a solved coefficient problem or a necessity theorem.

For a selected original minimizer, (3) plus sum_(i<n)h_i Q_star,i gives

    ||xn-xstar||²+2S(f_n-fstar)+B_terminal||g_n||²
          <=||x0-xstar||².

Equivalently, u_i=h_i for i<n andu_n=tau give a Zhang--Jiang dominance residual, with total weight

    sum u_i=S+tau=1+2kappa S.

To see this directly, use Q_star,i=2fstar-2f_i+2<g_i,x_i-xstar>-||g_i||² in the NONNEGATIVE interpolation convention above. The doubled dominance residual is

    ||x0-xstar||²-sum_i u_i Q_star,i
        -2(sum u_i)(f_n-fstar)-||x0-xstar-sum_i u_i g_i||².

Its exact expansion equals(3), because every cross-gradient coefficientu_i(h_j-u_j),j<i, vanishes. The minus sign in front ofsum u_i Q_star,i is essential with this nonnegative convention. Nonnegativity of(3) would thus imply the full-class value bound

    W_n(h)<=1/[1+(32/17)S] <=17/[16(1+2S)].

No such nonnegative combination for the complete inheritedH_m is supplied here. The positive-mode dictionary and the exact coefficient target must not be described as an upper certificate.

## 4. Required novelty and scope checks before any new allocation

The old R2 dense A/D diagonal-history family, even with arbitrary nonnegative diagonal slack and terminal penalty, was excluded on its stated scope. This mode dictionary must be compared with that actual family before being treated as a new route; no claim of strict containment or escape is made. The R7 all-level q_j terminal potential was actually false, so a reduced coefficient cannot simply be propagated by the primitive join theorem. The uniform B_terminal here is a distinct sufficient candidate, not a theorem or an approved repair.

A useful continuation would have to give actual nonnegative interval coefficients/slack on ALL relevant physical words, or show a sharp obstruction to this precise dictionary. Returning only the linear feasibility conditions above repeats an unproved certificate problem. This note carries NO new model, mathematical-program or formal allowance. The source's constant-step theorem, the Moreau/proximal objective criterion, and its balanced rate identity do not resolve the original Zhang--Jiang profile target.

## 5. Completed novelty check: the conic hull still has the central-bridge obstruction

After deriving Sections1--4, the parent read COMPLETE Sections2--4 of research/dense_history_search_20261008_r2/REPORT.md and its ROOT_DERIVATION_UNVERIFIED.md. Each interval mode above is an atom of that older A/D family. Specifically, extend

    A_k=sum_(a<=i<=min(k,b)) z_i, withA_k=0 beforea,
    D_k=sum_(max(k,a)<=i<=b) v_i, withD_k=0 afterb.

Then, for i<j,

    C_ji=(A_i-A_(i-1))(D_j+(h_j-1)D_(j+1)),
    C_ij=(A_(i-1)+(h_i-1)A_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)).        (4)

The terminal h_n is bookkeeping only; its term multipliesD_(n+1)=0. Weak monotonicity, including A_0=0 for intervals that start later, is enough. Formula(4) matches every original off-diagonal entry, not merely a terminal scalar.

A positive SUM of modes need not itself factor into one pairA,D. However the old central-cut proof extends to its entire finite positive conic hull. This requires a separate argument, given here rather than assuming that one-atom exclusion implies sum exclusion.

Index the atoms bynu and absorb their nonnegative combination weights intoA^nu. Define

    Z_k=sum_nu A_k^nu D_(k+1)^nu,
    q_k=1+2sum_nu A_(k-1)^nu D_k^nu,
    T_k=sum_(i<k)h_i,
    B_k^nu=sum_(i<k)h_i A_i^nu,
    E_k^nu=sum_(i>=k, i<n)h_i D_(i+1)^nu.

If the combined function coefficients are bvec_i=h_i fori<n andbvec_n=-S, the cut equations are exactly

    sum_nu[A_k^nu E_k^nu-D_(k+1)^nu B_k^nu
                         -2(h_k-1)A_k^nu D_(k+1)^nu]
       =T_k+h_k.                                       (5)

The physical diagonal residuals are

    mu_k=q_k-(h_k-1)^2 q_(k+1).

The same increment calculation as in the older prefix identity, now summing each bilinear atom product, proves

    q_k+sum_(i<k)mu_i
       =(T_k+1)^2-2sum_nu B_k^nu E_k^nu.               (6)

For clarity, B_(k+1)^nu=B_k^nu+h_k A_k^nu and E_(k+1)^nu=E_k^nu-h_k D_(k+1)^nu. The increment of the right side of(6), using(5), is[1-(h_k-1)^2]q_(k+1), exactly the increment on the left. Both sides start at1. Thus(6) needs only the COMBINED cut equations, not the false assumption that each atom separately matches them.

Since each B_k^nu E_k^nu>=0, nonnegative physical residuals implyq_k<=(T_k+1)^2. Now consider a palindrome with equal left/right masses s and middle physicalstep1+r, r>0, atindexk. SetC=s+1. Monotonicity givesE_(k+1)^nu<=s D_(k+1)^nu in EACH atom. The central cut(5), with the bridge separated from its right suffix, therefore gives

    C+r=sum_nu[A_k^nu E_(k+1)^nu+(1-r)A_k^nuD_(k+1)^nu
                                -D_(k+1)^nu B_k^nu]
         <=(C-r) Z_k.                                 (7)

The left side is positive, so C>r and Z_k>0. Hence

    q_(k+1)=1+2Z_k >=(3C+r)/(C-r).

At the same physical bridge, mu_k>=0 and(6) imply

    r² q_(k+1)<=q_k<=C².

With x=r/C in(0,1),

    x²(3+x)/(1-x)<=1,
    (x+1)(x²+2x-1)<=0,
    r/(s+1)<=sqrt2-1.                                 (8)

The weak inequality is sufficient. No strict-positivity assumption on every atom at every query was used.

The actual inherited central bridge violates(8). Withc0=1+sqrt2,epsilon=1/(4m),beta=1-epsilon,c=c0(1+epsilon),a=beta/c andj=m-1,

    r/(s+1)=(c-2)/[1+tau(2/c)^j],
    tau=[c(c-2)-beta]/beta,
    0<tau<9epsilon.

Forj>=3, (1/c0)*tau*(2/c)^j<72epsilon/c0^4<c0epsilon becausec0^5=41+29sqrt2>72. Sincec-2=1/c0+c0epsilon, this givesr/(s+1)>1/c0. Allm>=1536 havej>=3. Thus the entire positive conic hull of the variable-step interval modes is infeasible for the desired physical coefficient/diagonal-sign target, BEFORE imposing any terminal penalty.

This is a complete manual obstruction to the stated dictionary, not a full-PEP impossibility and not a counterexample to(3). It includes arbitrary finite mode sets, arbitrary positive z,v within intervals, arbitrary nonnegative combination weights, and arbitrary nonnegative physical diagonal slack. It does not include a signed mode expansion whose FINAL interpolation entries remain nonnegative, arbitrary nonseparable matrices not in this cone, non-diagonal PSD remainders, or different function-value weights.

No new model or mathematical program should be allocated to retuning POSITIVE weights in this dictionary. The source-derived algebra is valid, but its positive-sum route repeats the old central obstruction in a larger, now explicitly treated scope.

## Exact fixed-profile result

For each enclosing m>=1536, keep epsilon=1/(4m),beta=1-epsilon,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+epsilon),a=beta/c and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), with the SAME parameters in every child. LetS=sumH_m,T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t andBcert=1+(32/17)S. Bcert is not the original benchmarkB_n.

The fixed physical profiles were rho_i^A=1-T_i/(2S) andrho_i^B=(S+1)/(S+1+T_i). For either, u_i=rho_i h_i(i<n),u_n=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i. The original doubled dominance functional is

with NONNEGATIVEQ_star,i. It is strictly negative for BOTH fixed profiles on every sufficiently large ODD complete enclosing depthm, on one fixed scalar Huber function. Usef(x)=x²/2 for|x|<=1,|x|-1/2 otherwise, withuniqueoriginalminimum0 andgradientclip[-1,1]. Writek_j=a c^j,s_j=sumH_j and

    X1=beta, X_j=s_(j-1)+1+k_(j-1)-X_(j-1),j>=2,
    x0=X_m, x_n=g_n=(-1)^m t, t=a beta.

The complete positivity induction provesk_j>=X_j>0. Every leftchild/bridge is saturated with its current sign and lands at the negative next critical start.     V_m=sum h_i², M_m=sum h_i x_(i+1).

The source proofs provide exact finite-depth formulas and uniform geometric error bounds; no fixed-internal-depth limit is substituted. Profile A satisfies

    U_A=3S/4+V_m/(4S),

For PHYSICAL i<n only,

      =T_i(S-1-T_i)/[2S(S+1+T_i)]<=r²/2.

The last physicalstep>1 makes the index restriction valid. The ordering is reversed at terminalrho_n, which is not used to compare physical sums. Negative physical gradientmass/S tends(1-r)/2, yielding

    limsup_(oddm) Z_B/S < -19r/272<0.

ThusD_u=2X_m Z-Z²-Bcert t²<0. For any nonnegative original interpolation coefficientsC_ji with required row/column conditions,

    g^T R(C)g=D_u-sum C_ji Q_ij<=D_u<0.

The same ACTUAL gradient vector excludes FULLPSD for everyC; no diagonal restriction, old A/D ansatz, or free-vector screen is involved. The two independent derivations agree; that agreement is not Danus verifier acceptance.

Crucial scope: the actual objective payoff ist²/X_m²=Theta(S^-2), andS²payoff->1. It does NOT attain the targetS^-1 scale or refute a full-class upper boundW(H_m)<=1/Bcert. The failure is the profiles' prescribed residual square.

## Profile C and its necessary calibration

# Unallocated next lead: excess physical weights and a Laplacian remainder

**UNVERIFIED manual algebra; no complete certificate or new allocation.** The original Goal remains OPEN. This note defines a concrete alternative to the decreasing-clock profiles. It does not declare Profile C false or solve the original target.

## 1. A source-grounded motivation, with its exact boundary

In the canonical primitive--dominant concatenation of [Zhang--Jiang, Section3.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2), the left physical weights are the left steps, the bridge weight isgamma=s_left+2, and the right dominant vector is multiplied bytau>1. The physical bridge isbeta=1+(s_left+1)/tau<=gamma. Consequently the canonical recursively constructed dominant vector satisfiesu_i>=h_i at every PHYSICAL index. This is a property of those source-constructed schedules and weights; it does not make the actual perturbedH_m dominant.

The failed A/B profiles instead reduced physical weights and assigned the missing total to the terminal gradient. Their actual counterexample shows why that terminal residual square can be too large. The following fixed candidate puts more mass on physical gradients and less at the terminal query.

## 2. Explicit increasing-clock candidate

For the literal inheritedH_m letS=sumh,T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t andBcert=1+(32/17)S. Set

    u_i=(1+T_i/(2S))h_i, i<n,
    u_n=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i.                          (1)

The physical weights satisfyu_i>=h_i. WithV=sum h_i²,

    sum_(i<n)u_i=5S/4-V/(4S),
    u_n>=1+(43/68)S>0.

The strict inequality usesV>=0; no additional physical step is introduced. The desired total remainsBcert, distinct from the original benchmarkB_n.

Use NONNEGATIVEQ_star,i and define the original doubled dominance functional

    D_u=||x0-xstar||²-sum_i u_i Q_star,i
        -2Bcert(f_n-fstar)-||x0-xstar-sum_i u_i g_i||².

For any nonnegativeu, its exact gradient expansion is

    D_u=2sum_(i<n)u_i(f_i-f_n)
      +sum_i u_i(1-u_i)||g_i||²
      +2sum_(i<j)u_j(h_i-u_i)<g_i,g_j>.              (2)

Every h_i in the last sum is physical, becausei<j<=n.

## 3. Exact positive gradient-difference energy

Fori<j definew_ij=u_j(u_i-h_i)>=0. LetL be the graph Laplacian with off-diagonalL_ij=-w_ij and diagonalL_ii=sum_(j!=i)w_(min(i,j),max(i,j)). Then

    g^T L g=sum_(i<j) w_ij||g_i-g_j||²>=0.

The off-diagonal coefficients of(2) are EXACTLY those ofL. For any zero-row-sum interpolation matrixC with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sumsb_i=u_i(i<n),b_n=u_n-Bcert, putH=C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T using the ACTUAL trajectory matrix. The exact full remainder is

    R=L+diag(u_i(1-u_i)+b_i-L_ii)-H,
    D_u=sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij+g^T R g.                  (3)

This is a deterministic original-gradient identity. IfH is diagonal, the remainder is a positive gradient-difference energy plus a signed diagonal; nonnegative individual diagonal entries would be sufficient but are not assumed necessary. IfH is not diagonal, its whole contribution must be retained. NeitherL>=0 nor the mass identity in(1) provesR>=0.

Sinceu_i-[h_i,0]_i is componentwise nonnegative, the residual vector can also be written

    x0-sum_i u_i g_i=x_n-sum_i(u_i-[h_i,0]_i)g_i.

For projection envelopes, the latter excess-weighted gradient divided by its totalBcert-S is a genuine convex combination in the SAMEbody. This may provide a history-dependent comparison point. It is not an actual extra query or permission to change the function.

## 4. Exact critical-orbit calibration, not a validity claim

The complete symmetric-Huber orbit in the R12 A/B counterexample hasr=sqrt2-1,v=r²/(1+2sqrt2), X_m/S->r andM_m/S²->v, whereM_m=sum h_i x_(i+1). Generalizing(1) to u_i=(1+theta*T_i/S)h_i withtheta>0, finite summation by parts gives

At odd depths, whereg_n=x_n->-r, the totalZ=sumu_i g_i satisfies

For the fixedtheta1/2 in(1), both limits lie strictly between0 and2r. SinceBcert*f_n/S²->0, the leading dominance residual on this ONEorbit is positive at both parities. This proves only that the known A/B counterexample does not reject the new candidate. Other functions, starts and dimensions have not been checked.

A continuation must choose explicitC and prove the FULLmatrixR in(3) PSD, uniformly over every sufficiently large inheritedm, or produce a different actual complete-wordD_u<0 witness. A genuine fullPSDproof would implyW(H_m)<=1/Bcert and close the conditional original-target separation through accepted931b9653ce0f7201. No such proof exists in this note.

The old positive-interval-cone result fixed b_i=h_i and separately nonnegative physical diagonal slack; it cannot be transferred automatically to(3). # R12 concrete dominance profiles for the complete inherited word

**UNVERIFIED candidate functionals, not upper certificates.** These are fixed mathematical objects before the two R12 searches. No claim is made that their dominance residual is nonnegative. The original Goal remains OPEN.

For one enclosing m>=1536 keep epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, H0empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j). Let n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum h, T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, T_0=0,T_n=S. Use the SAME enclosing parameters in all children.

Let Bcert=1+(32/17)S. This is a proposed certificate weight, NOT the exact original benchmark B_n. The two profiles are:

    Profile A: rho_i=1-T_i/(2S), 0<=i<=n.
    Profile B: rho_i=(S+1)/(S+1+T_i), 0<=i<=n.

For either profile, set u_i=rho_i h_i fori<n and

    u_n=Bcert-sum_(i<n)u_i>0.

Indeed0<rho_i<=1 andBcert>S. Every weight is fixed by the entire physical word before a function is chosen. In Profile A, rho_0=1,rho_n=1/2, and the exact identity

    sum_(i<n)u_i=3S/4+sum_(i<n)h_i²/(4S)

follows from2sum h_i T_i=S²-sum h_i². The covariance is degenerate at the initial node because rho_0=1; a proof must handle that actual boundary, not replace it silently by a strict inequality. All other rho_i decrease strictly because the physical H_m steps are positive.

For any normalized convex1-smooth function with original selected minimizer x_star and actual GD path, define NONNEGATIVE interpolation rows

    Q_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||²,
    Q_star,i=2fstar-2f_i+2<g_i,x_i-xstar>-||g_i||².

The exact doubled dominance residual for either profile is

    D_u=||x0-xstar||²-sum_(i=0)^n u_i Q_star,i
        -2Bcert(f_n-fstar)-||x0-xstar-sum_(i=0)^n u_i g_i||².

It has no free function-dependent weights. A full-class proofD_u>=0 for every original trajectory would implyW(H_m)<=1/Bcert<=17/(16D_m), whereD_m=1+2S. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 would then give the desired strict asymptotic separation. Conversely, a legal trajectory withD_u<0 rejects that profile, not the full upper bound or the original minimax proposition.

For the deterministic covariance representation putell_i=h_i(i<n),ell_n=u_n/rho_n; ell_n is NOT a physical update. Define

    K_ij=ell_i ell_j[min(rho_i,rho_j)-rho_i rho_j].

It is PSD by the squared-integral identity in R11_BROWNIAN_BRIDGE_DOMINANCE_LEAD_20261009.md. With the ACTUAL trajectory matrixBmat_ij=h_j(j<i),Bmat_ii=1, choose zero-row-sumC with nonnegative off-diagonal entries and column sums

    b_i=u_i(i<n), b_n=-sum_(i<n)u_i=u_n-Bcert.

Then the exact full remainder is

    R=K+diag(u_i(1-ell_i)+b_i)-(C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T),
    D_u=sum_(i,j)C_ji Q_ij+g^T R g.

The concrete upper task is to constructC and proveR PSD for ALL sufficiently large inheritedm. The covariance alone is not enough. IfH=C Bmat+Bmat^T C^T is diagonal, signed entries ofR-K are allowed; replacing fullPSD by their separate nonnegativity would discard the new mechanism.

These profiles differ from the R3 last-prefix laws and from physical-weight positive-diagonal certificates. Their exact relation to any old obstruction must be checked, not guessed. No positive interval-mode retuning under the old b_i=h_i/nonnegative-diagonal assumptions is revived.

The independent primal search concerns these already fixed D_u functionals, not an unfinished C object from another search. It must produce actual fixed functions, actual trajectories, the original selected minimizer/radius, and a strict sign on an unbounded sequence of allowed enclosingm to reject an asymptotic profile. A finite-horizon toy or a freely assigned gradient table is not such a rejection.

# Parent R12 extension: every single nonnegative destination law fails

**UNVERIFIED manual proof. This excludes a coefficient family, not Profile C or the original minimax problem.** No new model or mathematical program was used. The R12b report checked one explicit rank-one flow; this note proves the stated larger single-law scope without changing that report's bytes.

Let the physical source weights be u_i>0 fori<n and letU=sum_(i<n)u_i. The required column sums areb_i=u_i physically andb_n=-U. Consider ANY matrix

    C=p q^T-diag(p), p_i>=0,q_i>=0,sum q_i=1,

whose column sums areb. WriteP=sum p_i. Then

    p_i=P q_i-u_i (i<n), p_n=P q_n+U,
    P>=U.

There is no restriction to q_i proportional to the algebraicell_i. Both p andq may depend arbitrarily on the whole word.

For a physical nodei letA_i=sum_(j<i)q_j andF_i=sum_(j>i)q_j. The FULL dominance remainder has diagonal

    R_ii=u_i(2-u_i)-2p_i[(h_i-1)F_i-A_i].          (1)

This follows from the actual lower-triangular trajectory matrix and already includes the complete covariance or other exact decomposition of the dominance cross terms.

Suppose along a sequence of growing words thatU->infinity, that there is a FIXED physical indexJ, and that for0<=i<=J:

- theu_i remain bounded;
- h_i-1 is bounded below by one positive constant;
- u_J>2 by one fixed positive margin for all sufficiently large words.

Then no such single-lawC can haveR PSD for all sufficiently large words. Proof follows.

AssumeR_ii>=0 for every0<=i<=J. Inductively prove thatp_i remains bounded for each such fixedi, or obtain a contradiction already at that node. Ifp_j is bounded forj<i, then

    P A_i=sum_(j<i)(p_j+u_j)=O(1),

andP>=U->infinity. The physical weight strictly afteri isU_after=U-sum_(j<=i)u_j, which tends to infinity. Nonnegativity ofp_j gives

    P F_i>=U_after.

Ifq_i>=1/2 along an unbounded subsequence, thenp_i=Pq_i-u_i>=P/2-O(1), whilep_i<=P. Hence

    2p_i[(h_i-1)F_i-A_i]
      >=(1-o(1)) r_min U_after-O(1) ->infinity.

This contradicts(1), becauseu_i(2-u_i) is bounded above. Thusq_i<1/2 eventually. SinceA_i=O(1/P), nowF_i=1-A_i-q_i>=1/2-o(1), and

    (h_i-1)F_i-A_i>=r_min/3>0

for all sufficiently large words. Equation(1) forcesp_i to be bounded whenu_i(2-u_i)>=0; if that latter number is negative, it already gives a contradiction. This proves the induction. AtindexJ, u_J>2 makesu_J(2-u_J)<0, so the contradiction necessarily occurs byJ.

For Profile C on the literal inheritedH_m, U>=7S_m/8->infinity. At each fixed early indexrho_i->1, henceu_i->h_i^0. The first four physical steps converge to

    sqrt2, 2, sqrt2, 1+(1+sqrt2).

Thus J=3 satisfies every hypothesis, includingu_3->2+sqrt2>2. The entire nonnegative single-destination-law class is therefore excluded at sufficiently large enclosingm, even ifq is retuned as a function ofm. The same argument applies to other clock profiles with these explicitly stated early-weight and total-source limits.

This does not exclude sums with different destination laws, general state-dependent circulations, arbitrary nonseparableC, or the possibility thatD_u>=0 has a different certificate. No negative direction here is asserted to be a realizable function trajectory. The result is only a necessary full-matrix diagnostic for the named coefficient family.

## New auxiliary result: the entire quadratic pool on the actual hard hierarchy

For fixed enclosing m>=4, set eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c, H0 empty and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j). Every child uses the SAME enclosing parameters. Let S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m, and

    p_m(lambda)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)[1-lambda(1+a*c^j)]^(2^(m-j-1)).

The parent draft proves, for every m>=4 and every lambda in[0,1],

    D_m*lambda*p_m(lambda)^2 <= 9/16.

The proof uses actual multiplicities and three frequency regimes: all factors positive, all negative, or the first negative level k. It proves S_j<=c^j-1 and S_j>=a*c^(j+1)/2. At a mixed-sign frequency, r=m-k, z=a*c^k*lambda and T=c*z*p_k(lambda) obey T<=2/e<3/4, z>a/(1+a), and |p_m|<=T^(2^r)/(z*c^(r+1)); the resulting bound is9/20. No frequency grid is used. Spectral decomposition extends the same quadratic-pool bound to finite-dimensional convex quadratics, retaining the original radius.

The parent freshly re-read accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 in this turn. It gives B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 for EVERY m>=1536 on precisely these parameters. Thus, at draft level, this word simultaneously has:

    standard noncrossing Huber witness = 1/D_m < (47/50)/B_n;
    full quadratic pool <=9/(16D_m) < (423/800)/B_n.

This closes the R5 missing scope check for that specific baseline portfolio. It does NOT optimize every Huber start or crossing itinerary, does NOT upper-bound unrestricted W(H_m), and does NOT disprove the original conjecture. The full-class upper W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) remains unproved.

## Common-normal replenishment: exact radius cost and a whole-word limitation

Use F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), K=conv(0,g_i), with every gradient query and the terminal sample retained. If an actual common normal e has <e,g_i>=1, then shifting EVERY point by t*e preserves all nonzero-vertex projection rows. It is legal exactly when every row against0 remains nonnegative. The changes are

    H->H+t, M->M+t, F_end->F_end+t,
    R^2->R^2+2t<x0,e>+t^2||e||^2.

Suppose the minimum-norm common-normal foot e/||e||^2 lies in conv(g_i). Writing g_i=w_i+q*nu, q=1/||e||, the tangential projections form another REAL static projection-gradient history for the SAME whole word, with0 in its gradient hull. If its original squared radius is D and its terminal twice-value is V, and the original normal coordinate is y, then

    W_origin=(V+2*q*y-(2S+1)*q^2)/(D+y^2)
       <=max{V/D,1/(2S+1)}.

The D=0 case is just the Huber term. This is an exact pointwise inequality, not an asymptotic approximation or a formal Gram direction. It follows from (y-(2S+1)q)^2>=0.

The complete minimum set is K-polar. Origin is closest precisely when x0 lies in cone(K). The native proved this for the R5 orthogonal histories and every legal replenishment, so their polar minimum cone gives no missing radius improvement. Their tangential generators are a centered simplex, generally NOT a regular simplex.

A second actual lift of the R5 centered simplex, using a shared component gamma in(0,1] and replenishment t>=0, retains every old/cross/minimum row and a closest origin. It obeys the same pointwise domination for EVERY underlying word and legal simplex parameter choice. More generally the foot condition holds for an independent gradient family with nonpositive cross inner products, by positivity of its Gram inverse applied to1; the closest-origin condition still needs a separate check outside the explicitly proved lift.

Therefore repeated common-normal shifts/lifts supply no improvement beyond the centered child or the standard Huber witness in these proven scopes. This does NOT upper-bound all centered children. Any closure through this mechanism must already obtain the needed value from its actual centered tangential history.

## Centered and path simplexes: what was genuinely selected, and what failed

The R5 centered simplex was completely rederived. With physical ordinary masses C_i-1, checkpoints1+d_i, A_i>0, sum1/A_i=1 and P_i=sum_(j>=i)A_j*C_j, feasibility is A_i*d_i>=P_(i+1). Put delta_i=d_i-P_(i+1)/A_i. Its exact data include

    barP=S+1-sum delta_i,
    2F_end=2sum delta_i+(A_M-1),
    R^2=sum_(i<j)(P_i-P_j)^2/(A_i*A_j).

All-tight guards force X_end=w_M. For a single checkpoint, the best legal ratio gives value d^2/[C_1^2(2C_2-1)], while all-tight selection gives d^2/(C_1^2 C_2^2); their ratio tends to0 as C_2 grows. Hence tight backward existence is not a quantitatively lossless selection theorem. This does not exclude the centered family with nonzero slacks.

The second line gives a new ACTUAL neighboring simplex with w1=a1*e1, wi=-bi*e_(i-1)+ai*ei, wM=-bM*e_(M-1). Set Q_i=C_i+d_i for2<=i<M and Q_M=ell>=C_M. The function is the same static projection envelope;0 is its unique minimum, start X0=C1*a1*e1, and every previous coordinate cancels at its checkpoint.     E_path=(d1/C1)^2*(2ell-2C_M+1)/ell^2
           *product_(i=2)^(M-1)[d_i*Q_(i+1)/(C_i*(Q_i+Q_(i+1)))].

No curvature or original-radius charge is omitted, but the product losses are real.

On the SAME inherited-m hierarchy, for EVERY m>=1536, EVERY nonempty subsequence of the rightmost root-to-leaf checkpoints, EVERY skipped-level pattern, any number of selected levels, EVERY ell>=C_tail and EVERY allowed amplitude choice in this exact-cancellation construction,

The proof explicitly includes skipped subtrees in physical phase masses. With V_j=1+S_j=alpha*c^j+(1-alpha)*2^j, alpha=a/(c-2), a level gap g before level r costs C>=c^(g-1)*V_r. # Uniform scalar-quadratic bound on the actual inherited-parameter word

Main-agent manual derivation, 2026-10-09 local time. **UNVERIFIED DRAFT**. No mathematical program, numerical array, solver, additional model or formal submission. This is an auxiliary baseline bound, not an upper bound for the full smooth-convex class and not a resolution of the original minimax problem.

## Statement

Fix an integer m>=4, and keep its parameters fixed throughout the word:

\[
 \varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\varepsilon,
 \quad c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c.
\]

Let H_0 be empty and H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j) for 0<=j<m. Write S_j=sum H_j and D_m=1+2S_m. For the scalar quadratic f(x)=lambda x^2/2, 0<=lambda<=1, define its actual GD polynomial

\[
 p_j(\lambda)=\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}
       [1-\lambda(1+a c^i)]^{2^{j-i-1}}.
\]

Then

\[
 \boxed{D_m\sup_{0\le\lambda\le1}\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2
       \le\frac9{16}.}                                      \tag{Q}
\]

The supremum is precisely the worst normalized objective over the scalar quadratic pool, with W=2R. It is also the supremum over all finite-dimensional convex quadratic functions with Hessian spectrum in [0,1], by spectral decomposition and the original minimizer-radius normalization. This last sentence does not extend (Q) to nonquadratic functions.

## Elementary parameter and mass bounds

For m>=4,

\[
 \frac{15}{16}\le\beta<1,\qquad
 \frac{12}{5}<c<\frac{21}{8},\qquad
 \frac5{14}<a<\frac12.
\]

Indeed c<=17(1+sqrt2)/16<21/8, c>=1+sqrt2>12/5, and a=beta/c. The endpoint rational comparisons follow by squaring positive quantities. Since c>=1+sqrt2 and beta<=1,

\[
 2+a\le c.
\]

The actual masses satisfy S_0=0 and S_{j+1}=2S_j+1+a c^j. Induction gives

\[
 S_j\le c^j-1,\qquad D_m<2c^m.                         \tag{1}
\]

Another induction, for j>=1, gives

\[
 S_j\ge\frac{a c^{j+1}}2.                              \tag{2}
\]

For j=1, a c^2/2=beta c/2<21/16<19/14<1+a=S_1. If (2) holds at j, then

\[
 2S_j+1+a c^j-\frac{a c^{j+2}}2
 \ge1+a c^j(1+c-c^2/2)>0,
\]

because 1+c-c^2/2>0 for c<=21/8. Thus (2) holds at j+1. Also S_m>=1.

## All frequencies, with no sampling

The bridge sizes b_i=1+a c^i strictly increase. Frequencies at a polynomial root give zero and require no special estimate. For every other lambda in (0,1], there are three cases.

### All factors positive

If lambda b_{m-1}<1, then 1-u<=exp(-u) yields

\[
 0<p_m(\lambda)\le e^{-\lambda S_m}.
\]

Therefore

\[
 D_m\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2
 \le\frac{2S_m+1}{2eS_m}
 \le\frac3{2e}<\frac9{16}.                            \tag{3}
\]

Here max_{t>=0} t exp(-2S_m t)=1/(2eS_m). The elementary bound e>8/3 follows from its positive power series (the sum through degree three is exactly 8/3).

### All factors negative

If lambda b_0>1, then for every i,

\[
 |1-\lambda b_i|=\lambda b_i-1\le b_i-1=a c^i.
\]

The finite geometric-sum identity gives

\[
 |p_m(\lambda)|
 \le a^{2^m-1}c^{2^m-m-1}
 =\beta^{2^m-1}c^{-m}\le c^{-m}.
\]

By (1),

\[
 D_m\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2<2c^{-m}
 \le2(5/12)^4<\frac9{16}.                            \tag{4}
\]

### The first negative level is k with 1<=k<m

Then lambda b_i<1 for i<k and lambda b_i>1 for i>=k. Put

\[
 r=m-k\ge1,\qquad z=a c^k\lambda,\qquad
 T=c z p_k(\lambda).
\]

All factors defining p_k are positive, so (2) gives

\[
 T=a c^{k+1}\lambda p_k(\lambda)
 \le a c^{k+1}\lambda e^{-S_k\lambda}
 \le \frac{a c^{k+1}}{eS_k}\le\frac2e<\frac34.       \tag{5}
\]

Moreover lambda<1/b_0=1/(1+a), while lambda b_k>1. Consequently

\[
 z>1-\lambda>\frac{a}{1+a}.                           \tag{6}
\]

The higher-level factors obey |1-lambda b_i|<=lambda a c^i. Keeping their exact multiplicities,

\[
 |p_m(\lambda)|
 \le p_k(\lambda)^{2^r}
       z^{2^r-1}c^{2^r-r-1}
 =\frac{T^{2^r}}{z c^{r+1}}.                          \tag{7}
\]

Combining (1), (6), (7), and a c=beta,

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 D_m\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2
 &<\frac{2T^{2^{r+1}}}{a z c^{r+2}}\\
 &<\frac{2(1+a)}{\beta^2}\,
          c^{-r}T^{2^{r+1}}\\
 &<\frac{3}{(15/16)^2(12/5)}\,(3/4)^4
 =\frac9{20}<\frac9{16}.                              \tag{8}
 \end{aligned}
\]

The last step uses r>=1, c>1 and 0<=T<3/4. This covers every remaining lambda. At lambda=0 the normalized objective is zero. Equations (3)--(8) prove (Q) within this draft.

## What this buys, conditionally on the accepted benchmark comparison

For exactly this same enclosing-m word, accepted fact 931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_{2^m-1}/D_m<47/50 for m>=1536. If the present draft is correct, then

\[
 B_{2^m-1}\sup_{\lambda\in[0,1]}\lambda p_m(\lambda)^2
 <\frac{423}{800},
\]

and the standard noncrossing Huber witness on the same word gives B_{2^m-1}/D_m<47/50. Thus this actual asymptotic word fails to reach the exact target through the portfolio consisting of that standard Huber witness and all convex quadratics. This is not an optimization over every Huber scale, start or crossing itinerary. It fills a previously missing scope check when testing this specific baseline portfolio.

It does not imply W(H_m)<=9/(16D_m), or W(H_m)<=1/D_m, or a negative answer to the original problem. A nonlinear adversary may have a much larger last-iterate objective. No formal review has been submitted.

# R7 direct-proof handoff: Goal ACTIVE, original target OPEN

2026-10-09 local time. Read current OPERATOR.md and COMPLETE original PROBLEM.md before new work. The latest human request remains direct original-target closure inspired by Zhang--Jiang, independent judgment, and fewer equivalent formulations or fixed-horizon toys. All model-backed research is gpt-6-astra/ultra, consult OFF. The historical Goal text says max, but the later explicit ultra preference and current OPERATOR control calls.

## Fixed target and live authority

For every finite-dimensional convex L-smooth function with an attained minimizer, original selected minimizer/start, actual final iterate, and predetermined nonnegative word h, retain

    W_n(h)=sup 2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x0-x_star||^2), W=2R.

Prove lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1, equivalently a word-independent N_epsilon for every epsilon>0 such that every n>=N_epsilon and every h>=0 satisfies W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n; or produce a certified full-function-class asymptotic separation. The exact benchmark keeps EVERY integer split k+l=n-1:

    C0=B0=1,
    C_n=max Omega(C_k,C_l), B_n=max G(C_k,B_l), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x*x+6*x*y+y*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y*y+8*x*y)/2.

The original PROBLEM SHA remains3ecc3159a696e8528ebc727776d42f657cfbc6842e9212b28c870fcd05edb89d. The live Goal was ACTIVE with unbounded budget when R7 began. No pause request or external blocker arose; mathematical incompleteness is not a reason to change that status.

## R7 allocations and final artifacts

Both native calls finished; the parent read each COMPLETE proof and handoff and checked final hashes. All new mathematics is UNVERIFIED under Danus policy. There is no complete original-target candidate for formal review.

Its prose prefixes the ID with the word grant; the allocation ID itself is the16-character value above.
- relaxed_terminal_potential_20261009_r7, native of the same name, allocation9551ad5108ac3aae: ONE invocation, ONE substantive line, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal0. Optional second line was deliberately unused, not carried forward. PROOF_ATTEMPT SHA79a8eb9e5ee5b4912445562b33ee634178b5118955cba73805f7efe7c34d052d; HANDOFFd2882a6930b01d8366256f0c3931c1e478c2d1d8864edc73c38405b254ba2c74.

All R3--R6 calls remain closed. No old worker, numerical array or mathematical budget was reopened. No additional model/agent beyond these two was used in R7. No formal request, publication, finalization, revocation or Goal mutation occurred.

## The same actual hierarchy and its evidence boundary

For enclosing integer m, keep eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c fixed throughout H0 empty and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j). Let S_j=sum H_j, V_j=1+S_j, D_m=1+2S_m, n_m=2^m-1.

Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 for every m>=1536. Its full-class premise W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) remains UNPROVED. Proving that bound for every sufficiently large m would already give the uniform strict gap799/800 on an unbounded subsequence and refute the original limit-one statement; starting exactly at1536 is not essential to an asymptotic disproof.

R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md, SHAb173601dd4fb908b0a3f4ef47003f3df78eb2687a3dd7094f9f6ce0c02e97ffa, remains an UNVERIFIED complete manual draft. It bounds the WHOLE convex-quadratic pool by9/(16D_m), m>=4. The baseline portfolio in use consists of that pool and the specified standard noncrossing Huber witness1/D_m. It does not optimize every Huber cap/start/crossing, and it does not upper-bound unrestricted nonlinear W.

A fresh main fact_search for asymmetric Huber/crossing retrieved b98bb807d514e80d: its accepted single-cut portfolio is

    max{1/(1+2S), max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+prefix_mass_j)^2(1+2tail_mass_j)]}.

That single-cut mechanism was already asymptotically insufficient. Saved current receipt: research/r7_huber_scope_raw.jsonl. R7 did not renew it as a new lower-bound mechanism.

## Direct upper attempt: the all-level relaxed potential is FALSE

The concrete candidate was, for every level j<=m and every actual H_j trajectory,

    ||x_end-x_star||^2+2S_j(f_end-f_star)
       +q_j S_j(S_j+1)||g_end||^2 <= ||x_start-x_star||^2,
    q_j=(1+eps)^(-j).                                      (P_j)

If the terminal P_m were true, it would close the accepted negative route. The native instead constructed a real counterexample to P7 for EVERY enclosing m>=1536, with no numerical sampling and no restriction to one dual-certificate ansatz.

Set A=1+S6, d=a*c^6, r=d/A. Use the single globally convex1-smooth function

    f_r(x)=-r*x-r^2/2 for x<=-r,
            x^2/2 for -r<=x<=1,
            x-1/2 for x>=1.

Its derivative is clamp(x,-r,1), its unique minimum is0, and the original start is x0=A. The first complete H6 stays on positive gradient1 and ends at1. The bridge1+d lands at-d. The second complete H6 stays on gradient-r and ends at-r. Thus final x=g=-r, f=r^2/2 and original radius squared A^2, for the literal127-step wordH7. For any q, the joint terminal left side divided by A^2 equals

    q[r(2+r)]^2+(1-q)r^2(2+r)/A.

The report proves exactly, for0<eps<=1/6144,

    r>sqrt2-1+(4/3)eps,
    q7[r(2+r)]^2>1+(7/15)eps(1-112eps)>1.

Its second summand is also positive. The proof uses V_j=v*c^j+(1-v)2^j, v=(1-eps)/[c(c-2)], and an explicit rational bound on the resulting expression for r; all signs and integer comparisons are recorded. This is a genuine rejection of the UNIVERSAL ALL-LEVEL premise. It is NOT a counterexample to terminal P_m at m>=1536, to every altered coefficient sequence, or to the full objective upper W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m).

The native independently checked the final extraction. If an actual full-class inequality has the form ||z||^2+2A*F+B||g||^2<=R0^2, with A,B>=0 and F the objective gap, smooth interpolation and Cauchy--Schwarz give

    W<=2/[2A+1+sqrt(4B+1)].

Also q_m>exp(-1/4)>225/289=(15/17)^2. A rational series proof is included: e^(1/4)<37473/29184<289/225. Hence a separately proved P_m really would yield17/(16D_m). The failure is in the proposed all-level induction, not the extraction or W=2R normalization.

A fixed finite startup penalty could remove this particular obstruction and would still tend to exp(-1/4) at j=m. That observation is NOT a valid composition rule or a new theorem. The call stopped after ONE substantive line instead of spending the second allowance on an unsupported cap adjustment. Do not automatically renew scalar-cap tuning.

## Returning lower line 1: the specified two-curvature family is no stronger than quadratics

For0<mu<=1, take f_mu(x)=x^2/2 on x>=0 and mu*x^2/2 on x<=0. This is one static convex1-smooth function, unique min0, with every original nonzero start included. Define mu0=(1-a)/(1+a).

For mu<=mu0, negative-side visits at level0 cannot become positive. Every positive visit occurs at a level0 step, either initially or just after a larger bridge; that step immediately returns negative. Its multiplier a is <=1-mu*(1+a), while every negative-side multiplier is the actual curvature-mu quadratic multiplier. The final point is nonpositive, giving

    E_mu(H_m)<=mu*p_m(mu)^2.

For mu>=mu0, every multiplier on either side has magnitude<=d_j=a*c^j at levelj, hence

    E_mu(H_m)<=P_m^2=p_m(1)^2,
    P_m=beta^(2^m-1)*c^(-m).

Thus the entire stated two-curvature family is dominated by the actual scalar-quadratic pool on the SAME full word and original radius. Under the R6 auxiliary draft it is <=9/(16D_m). This is a whole-family comparison before invoking that numerical constant.

There really are repeated returns: mu=1/2 and a positive start give exactly2^(m-2)-1 negative-to-positive switches, each followed by a positive-to-negative return, for every m>=4. The comparison includes coordinate reflection. It does NOT cover all pairs of curvatures strictly below1, all one-dimensional convex functions, or the full function class. Do not renew this exact family as though the existence of returns supplied a new gain.

## Returning lower line 2: the full affine-terminal sector of a one-sided Huber function is closed

Use F(x)=max_(g>=-1)(x*g-g^2/2), which is x^2/2 for x>=-1 and -x-1/2 for x<=-1. It is convex1-smooth with unique min0. Its literal map is T_h(x)=(1-h)x on x>=-1 and x+h on x<=-1. Every finite orbit is exactly representable by one compact asymmetric interval hull[-1,M] with M chosen before the run above every visited gradient.

The parent supplied, and the native independently derived, an exact original-radius bookkeeping device. Starting from the ENTIRE interval[-R,R], continuity and fixed point0 make each actual image an interval[-A_t,B_t]. For h_t>1 and delta_t=h_t-1,

    A_(t+1)=max{A_t-h_t,delta_t*B_t},
    B_(t+1)=delta_t*min{A_t,1}, A0=B0=R.

Every point in the final interval is reachable by an actual original start of radius<=R. Optimizing the terminal endpoint energy divided by R^2 is exactly the supremum over original starts, not a favorable renormalization. The recurrence itself is not a solved quantitative theorem.

The native then proved a quantitative whole-word envelope, including every occurrence in every subtree and ANY number of returns:

    A_n(R)<=max{R-S_m, aR-[S_m-(1+a)], a*beta}, B_n(R)<=a.

Proof: unroll every reflected contribution delta_t*B_t minus the entire remaining suffix mass; B_t<=delta_(t-1). Adjacent high/low levels bound the reflected contribution by a*d_j. A level-j bridge has an entire H_j after it, and its following leaf still has suffix mass S_j-(1+a). The induction S_j-(1+a)>=a*d_j for j>=2 pays every high-level excursion; level1 leaves only a*beta. All physical suffixes are retained; there is no selected-spine restriction.

It follows exactly that A_n(R)>=1 iff R>=S_m+1, and A_n(R)=R-S_m in that region. Every actual affine-terminal trajectory, however many times it previously returned, obeys

    2F(x_n)/|x0|^2 <= [2R-D_m]/R^2 <=1/D_m,
    R=|x0|,

with equality at x0=-D_m. The entire affine-terminal sector therefore has exact optimum1/D_m. Every R>=sqrt(D_m) is also bounded by1/D_m. For R<=1, every physical prefix stays quadratic, so the exact value is P_m^2<1/D_m.

The UNSOLVED region for this fixed one-sided function is the quadratic terminal sector with1<R<sqrt(D_m). With complete physical interval endpoints, write

    K_m=sup_(1<R<sqrt(D_m)) max{A_n(R)^2,B_n(R)^2}/R^2.

The proven sector bounds give E_F(H_m)=max{1/D_m,K_m}. No target-scale lower bound, and no full-family upper, for K_m was obtained. Merely naming K_m is not progress toward the minimax target. Even a successful liminf B_n*K_m>=1 would only pass this inherited-family viability test; the original arbitrary-word selection would still need proof. The whole finite-cap asymmetric-Huber family and higher-dimensional returning histories remain unexcluded.

## Parent manual bridge algebra retained for source fidelity, not a claimed closure

The parent manually derived the following identities during R7; they were sent to the upper call, but that call did not use them to claim a second proof line. They remain UNVERIFIED and must be checked before reuse.

For a word of massS define the history functional

    H_B=sum_i h_i[2(f_i-f_end)+||g_i||^2+2<g_i,x_start-x_i>]
          -||x_end-x_start||^2-B||g_end||^2.

For (left,alpha,right), child masses s,t, left endpoint a with gradientv, and final endpointm,

    H_Bnew(global)=H_Bleft(left)+H_Bright(right)
       +2(s+alpha)(f_a-f_m)+(Bleft+alpha-alpha^2)||v||^2
       +(Bright-Bnew)||g_m||^2.

With Q_ij=2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2>=0, the nonnegative combination

    2H_Bright+sum_right h_i(Q_ai+Q_mi)+Q_am+(alpha-1)Q_ma

equals

    2(t+2-alpha)(f_a-f_m)
      -(t+3alpha-2alpha^2)||v||^2-(t+2Bright+alpha)||g_m||^2.

Thus, if the right history functional is actually nonnegative and1<=alpha<t+2, this yields the corresponding generalized multistep decrease. It does NOT establish nonnegativity of the relaxed parent functional or composition of arbitrary endpoint potentials. Simply discarding child/cross residuals loses precisely information a new proof would need. No new universal dual-equivalence theorem was submitted or assumed.

## Next-action judgment and recovery

Do not rerun the exact q_j all-level candidate, the exact unit-positive-curvature two-sided family, common-normal replenishment covered by R6, or the unchanged rightmost-spine path product. The one-sided intermediate-radius sector is a concrete unresolved question; the final P_m is another. Neither is an established viable route. No original-target proof or disproof was obtained, and no candidate merits a formal call yet.

## Actual tangent disk and quantitative sector bounds

For K=B(e1,1) in R2, define F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2). It is one global convex1-smooth function with gradient P_K and complete minimum set{(z,0):z<=0}. For x=(1-u,v), u>=1, write r=sqrt(u^2+v^2), delta=r-u, q=r+u. The ORIGINAL closest minimum is(1-u0,0), so the initial radius squared is v0^2. Actual updates and objective are

    F=delta, g=(delta/r,v/r),
    u'=u+h*delta/r, v'=(1-h/r)*v,
    r'^2=r^2+2h*delta*(h/r-1), q'>=q,
    E=2F_end/v0^2=(2/q_end)*product(1-h_t/r_t)^2.

All radii in the product are coupled states of the one fixed function. The original-radius debt is ||x_t-x_star||^2=(r_t-u0)^2+2u0*delta_t. Supporting normals are exactly0.5(r_i-1)||g_i-g_j||^2, so old and future rows are genuine. Tangential curvature is1/r.

The native proves for EVERY complete finite nonnegative word with S=sum h>0 and b=max h, every left-side start with q0>=2b obeys

    E <=(2/q0)*exp(-2S/r0)<=1/(eS).

Here q monotonicity gives r_t>=b; consequently there is no reflection, r_t decreases, and every multiplier is controlled. On the exact H_m, S_m>3 gives D_m E<7/8 and hence B_n E<329/400. This controls the entire stated start sector. Failure of its sufficient condition does not imply that reflection occurs.

There is an explicit changing-curvature trajectory on every H_m: u0=v0=3S_m. Its r_t strictly decreases and q_t strictly increases at every physical step, and E>=4/(27sqrt2 S_m). This is only a subtarget constant, inside the deficient sector; it is not a viable target witness.

For each FIXED word and u0>=1, the limit v0->0 is exactly (1/u0)*product(1-h_t/u0)^2. It recovers the quadratic pool. Conditional on the R6 draft, fixed-m uniform continuity over1<=u0<=b_m gives some eta_m>0 with E<=3/(4D_m) when0<|v0|<=eta_m. No quantitative or uniform-in-m eta_m was proved.

The SAME disk already supplies the standard Huber witness on its positive horizontal axis. The function there is0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for0<=z<=2, and2z-2 forz>=2. The start x0=2D_m e1 has original closest minimum0 and payoff1/D_m. Because every H_m step exceeds1, every axis trajectory entering the quadratic interval before the terminal time reaches the minimum ray at its next step. Checking the possible final quadratic visit proves the exact optimum over ALL horizontal-axis starts is1/D_m. The radius v0^2 must not be used for a positive-axis start.

Still OPEN: finite-amplitude left starts with q0<2b_m, and positive-horizontal-coordinate nonaxis starts including their actual interior/exterior transitions. No whole-disk upper or target-scale lower was obtained. The optional vertex-plus-disk line was not used: a plateau already exists, and no quantified repair was available. The line stopped instead of producing another bare geometric parametrization.

## Area argument: correct invariant, but an actual positive-area bad sector

In p=(u,v) coordinates, T_h(p)=h e1+(1-h/r)p. Away from v=0 and r=h,

    det DT_h=1-h/r=v'/v,

so each chart(u,log|v|) has determinant1 and the measure du dv/|v| is preserved. The inverse branch is determined by sign(u'-h): for Rb=||p'-h e1||, r=h+Rb on u'>h and r=h-Rb on u'<h, with corresponding p=(1+h/Rb)(p'-h e1) or(1-h/Rb)(p'-h e1). The second branch needs Rb<h, and both must satisfy originalu>=1. Reflection changes the sign chart and causes no extra multiplicity.

The circle r=h collapses to(h,0), an actual minimum; first-hit preimages are null off the axis. The report gives the full image-domain bounds, not just a Jacobian. On any initial finite log-height band, the terminal region compatible with arbitrarily small payoff extends to log|v|=-infinity and has infinite invariant area. Positive preserved area alone therefore supplies no contradiction.

More decisively, the R8a report constructs an ACTUAL positive-area reflecting sector on every inherited H_m. Put ell_j=1+a*c^j, zeta=1/64, eta0=zeta^2/8, and choose

    p0=(ell0+delta)*(cos theta,sin theta),
    eta0*ell0/2 <=delta<=eta0*ell0,
    3/4<=cos theta<=4/5, sin theta>0.

It has u0>1, 4/5<|v0|<3, and invariant area

    (eta0*ell0/2)*integral_[acos(4/5),acos(3/4)] csc(theta)dtheta,

bounded below by a positive constant independent of m. The first leaf sends it exactly to(ell0+delta cos theta,delta sin theta). A complete-word bootstrap proves ell0<r_t<ell0*(1+zeta/2) thereafter. Thus every later leaf multiplier is<zeta and every nonleaf level j reflects with multiplier magnitude<=c^j. No point collapses.

The proof retains EVERY physical prefix through

    Q_j=zeta^(2^(j-1))*c^(2^j-j-1)
       =c^(-j-1)*d^(2^(j-1)), d=zeta*c^2<1/4,
    J_j=sum h_t*(formal prefix product)^2<3.

The exact recursion J_(j+1)=(1+A_j)J_j+ell_j Q_j^2, A_j=c^(2j)Q_j^2, and sum A_j<1/15 pays the actual horizontal drift and closes the uniform bootstrap. No array or numerical calculation was used. The ORIGINAL-radius payoff satisfies, at EVERY point of this sector,

    E <4^(-2^m),
    D_m*sup_sector E <11*3^m*4^(-2^m) ->0.

This is not a full-disk bound. It is a concrete reason not to treat area preservation, bounded initial radius and actual reflections as sufficient for target-scale selection. A new area argument would need quantitative control of the actual terminal image and its dependence on the original radius, outside this bad sector. No such selection estimate was found. Do not log the invariant itself as a minimax result.

## Matched-child U attempt: exact retained rows, no quantitative payment

For the actual two copies of H_j, let x_i,y_i and g_i=P(x_i), f_i=P(y_i), with y0=x_N-b*g_N. Put delta_i=x_i-y_i and e_i=g_i-f_i. The original matched normal sum is mu_i=<delta_i,e_i>-||e_i||^2>=0. It gives

    ||delta0||^2-||deltaN||^2
      =sum h_i(2-h_i)||e_i||^2+2sum h_i mu_i,

with actual bridge boundaries delta0=sum h_i g_i+b*g_N, deltaN=sum h_i f_i+b*g_N. The long-step terms have negative coefficients. Combining them with the accepted root U identity leaves the exact unpaid term in report(3.6), including the real right radial/maximization remainders with NEGATIVE signs. Their favorable values cannot be chosen independently.

The difference data are not automatically a convex gradient history. Their putative normal row is

    S_ij=Q_ij^(L,L)+Q_ij^(R,R)-Q_ij^(R,L)-Q_ij^(L,R)-mu_j,

which has no justified sign even though every original Q<=0. Thus no Silver child certificate was applied in a fictitious difference space.

The second line keeps the actual left auxiliary l=P(x/A), with A=1+2s, child capacityC=1+s, bridgeb=1+k, D=d+2s, h=C+k, M=D+2h, r=P(w/D), zL=[x-v+C p]/A and u=l-zL. Its parent point is

    z_theta=zP+(theta*A/M)u,
    M z_theta=(1-theta)sum h_i g_i+(b-theta C)p+(D+h)r+theta A l.

Convexity proves feasibility for0<=theta<=b/C<1. The exact parent gap(4.5) contains actual child U gaps and nonnegative root/parent reserves, but also

    T_theta=-(1-theta)^2 A^2||u||^2
       -2(1-theta)A<x-A l,u>
       +2theta A<(s-k)p-(D+h)r,u>.

The normal<x-A l,u> is nonnegative but has a negative coefficient. Taking theta1 cancels an earlier remainder coefficient yet makes bridge weight b-C=k-s negative; no actual same-body membership of the extrapolated point was proved. The root square controls p-r but not the leftover r component. The positive root coefficient itself is not the problem: Theta>3C(C+k)/46 at all relevant higher levels. No all-depth certificate, uniform controlled loss, or actual counterexample to U was obtained.

These two signed identities identify an unpaid term; they are not a replacement theorem of equal strength and do not establish minimax progress by themselves. The next upper attempt needs an actual quantitative payment with all correlated child data.

## Research judgment and unallocated leads

Do not renew the bare area-preservation argument, rederive the disk recurrence, retune the already bounded nonreflecting/axis sectors, assume a difference-space objective, or declare theta1 feasible from convexity. None supplies the original target.

A more promising lower search, not yet allocated or proved, is the complete NONVERTEX evolution in a fixed weighted centered simplex. On a finite probability space with positive fixed weights p_i, use the mean-zero L2 space and K={g:E g=0,g_i>=-1}. This is a compact centered simplex. Projection has g_i=(X_i-t)_+-1, where t is uniquely chosen by E(X-t)_+=1. Thus one fixed function gives the actual update X'=X-h*g, preserving E X=0 and the original squared radius E X0^2. Its objective is F=t+1/2+E(X-t)_+^2/2. These parent manual starting formulas require independent checking before use; they are NOT a new lower bound or a universality theorem for arbitrary functions.

The potential advantage is simultaneous nonvertex face mixtures with fixed atom weights, rather than the vertex-by-vertex itineraries previously bounded. The starting law must be selected ONCE from the full prescribed word, and then all physical steps propagated without resetting atoms or child states. A useful new call must actually choose such a finite law and prove the original-scale payoff, first on the inherited family and then uniformly on all words. Merely outputting this probability reformulation would repeat the old gap. The existing R5 centered-simplex construction and R6/R7 restrictions are prerequisites, not proofs about this full nonvertex law.

For an upper attempt, one unallocated possibility is to quantify what fraction of an ACTUAL child maximization remainder is retained by its U gap. The relation E_L=V_L/A+RL suggests testing a genuinely proved relative-debt bound before trying another scalar terminal profile. No coefficient, base case or composition theorem for such a bound is known. It must not be assumed, and a clipped/extrapolated comparison point must have actual membership and all correction terms proved. These are directions, not results or renewed grants.

The parent also considered an interior shifted disk B(b e1,1),0<b<1, whose unique minimum and reversible horizontal drift differ from the tangent-ray geometry. No trajectory/payoff theorem or allocation was made for it. Its existence alone is not evidence that it can close the target.

## Primary-source refresh and operating state

A bounded new primary-source search did not supply a theorem resolving this exact minimax profile. This was not a comprehensive absence-of-literature claim. The parent inspected [Peppy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35762), posted September28, and its [Sections2.3--2.5 and AppendicesC/D](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.35762). It extracts low-rank partial-sum Lyapunov structure from full PEP certificates and then completes analytic identities. The inspected examples concern specified algorithms such as FGM; no solution to the present Silver minimax question was imported. No Peppy code, model workflow, solver or old numerical array was run. Its method is only a possible guide to a future freshly bounded certificate search.

Initial cached gm_search metadata extraction used the wrong nesting and returned nulls; the cached result was then parsed correctly without another RPC. A broad cached print was truncated; only the relevant public claim/evidence record was copied, and the native read that complete copy. A guessed R3 handoff alias was absent; the current R7 handoff and actual accepted Part B/C were used instead. No private local-memory link was followed. There was no approval-review rejection.

All R8/R8a work is closed after the receipt. Original Goal remains ACTIVE/OPEN, with no complete candidate for formal verification and no mathematical/program/formal allowance carried forward. Any next call requires a fresh bounded grant under the standing human authorization.
